[Email 5] Re: ESCALATION: BigCorp account - API timeout issuesscore 0.808
From: Bob Martinez <bob@acme.com>
To: Raj Gupta <raj@acme.com>
Date: Wed, 12 Feb 2025 14:30:00 +0000
<escalation-reply-027@test.example.com>
On February 11, 2025, at 3:05 AM UTC, the production server in us-east-1 experienced a complete outage with all customer-facing APIs returning 503 errors. Monitoring data indicated that CPU usage had spiked to 100% and database connections were exhausted.

Dave Wilson notified Bob Martinez about the issue since no response was received from the on-call team [Email 1]. Upon investigation, Bob identified an issue in a batch job deployed on Friday which caused connection leaks, opening 50 DB connections per run without releasing them. He restarted the app server and increased the connection pool limit to 200 to restore API functionality temporarily [Email 2].

Dave confirmed that the missing 'finally' block in the nightly analytics batch job was causing the leak since its deployment on February 7th. A fix for this was deployed to staging, and a pull request (#847) was created for the permanent solution before the next nightly run [Email 3]. The incident was fully resolved by deploying the fix in production at 14:30 UTC.

A postmortem documented that the outage lasted for 45 minutes with an additional 12 minutes of degraded performance. A prevention measure included setting up alerts when connection pool capacity reaches 80% [Email 4].
Session tail (2 messages)
user:
Walk me through the production server incident. What happened?
assistant:
On February 11, 2025, at 3:05 AM UTC, the production server in us-east-1 experienced a complete outage with all customer-facing APIs returning 503 errors. Monitoring data indicated that CPU usage had spiked to 100% and database connections were exhausted.

Dave Wilson notified Bob Martinez about the issue since no response was received from the on-call team [Email 1]. Upon investigation, Bob identified an issue in a batch job deployed on Friday which caused connection leaks, opening 50 DB connections per run without releasing them. He restarted the app server and increased the connection pool limit to 200 to restore API functionality temporarily [Email 2].

Dave confirmed that the missing 'finally' block in the nightly analytics batch job was causing the leak since its deployment on February 7th. A fix for this was deployed to staging, and a pull request (#847) was created for the permanent solution before the next nightly run [Email 3]. The incident was fully resolved by deploying the fix in production at 14:30 UTC.

A postmortem documented that the outage lasted for 45 minutes with an additional 12 minutes of degraded performance. A prevention measure included setting up alerts when connection pool capacity reaches 80% [Email 4].

[Email 5] Re: ESCALATION: BigCorp account - API timeout issuesscore 0.828
From: Bob Martinez <bob@acme.com>
To: Raj Gupta <raj@acme.com>
Date: Wed, 12 Feb 2025 14:30:00 +0000
<escalation-reply-027@test.example.com>
The fix for the production server outage was implemented through a series of steps:

1. Bob identified that the root cause was a connection leak in a batch job deployed on Friday, which opened 50 database connections per run without releasing them [Email 2].

2. To address the immediate issue, Bob restarted the application server and increased the connection pool limit to 200 temporarily, restoring API functionality [Email 2].

3. A permanent fix was developed by Dave Wilson who confirmed that the missing 'finally' block in the nightly analytics batch job was causing the leak since its deployment on February 7th [Email 3]. The fix was deployed to staging and a pull request (#847) was created for production deployment before the next nightly run.

4. Finally, the permanent fix was deployed to production at 14:30 UTC, fully resolving the outage [Email 4].

The impact of these actions on services included:
- The server went down with all customer-facing APIs returning 503 errors.
- Monitoring data indicated that CPU usage had spiked to 100% and database connections were exhausted.
- The incident lasted for approximately 45 minutes, followed by an additional 12 minutes of degraded performance.

A postmortem was conducted summarizing the cause, impact, fix, and prevention measures such as setting up alerts when connection pool capacity reaches 80%. [Email 4]

user:
Walk me through the production server incident. What happened?
assistant:
On February 11, 2025, at 3:05 AM UTC, the production server in us-east-1 experienced a complete outage with all customer-facing APIs returning 503 errors. Monitoring data indicated that CPU usage had spiked to 100% and database connections were exhausted.

Dave Wilson notified Bob Martinez about the issue since no response was received from the on-call team [Email 1]. Upon investigation, Bob identified an issue in a batch job deployed on Friday which caused connection leaks, opening 50 DB connections per run without releasing them. He restarted the app server and increased the connection pool limit to 200 to restore API functionality temporarily [Email 2].

Dave confirmed that the missing 'finally' block in the nightly analytics batch job was causing the leak since its deployment on February 7th. A fix for this was deployed to staging, and a pull request (#847) was created for the permanent solution before the next nightly run [Email 3]. The incident was fully resolved by deploying the fix in production at 14:30 UTC.

A postmortem documented that the outage lasted for 45 minutes with an additional 12 minutes of degraded performance. A prevention measure included setting up alerts when connection pool capacity reaches 80% [Email 4].

Project Falcon is a project with confirmed launch date initially set for March 15, 2025. This was communicated in [Email 4] by Alice Chen on February 5, 2025. However, the marketing team requested a two-day delay, shifting the new launch date to March 17, 2025 as stated in [Email 3] sent by Alice Chen on February 13, 2025.

Bob Martinez acknowledged this and confirmed the QA readiness deadline in his response to Alice [Email 5]. He also mentioned scheduling a dry run for March 14 (originally March 12) and indicated that the staging environment was already set up and passing basic smoke tests.

In addition, Dave Wilson proposed a PostgreSQL upgrade from PG14 to PG16 before Falcon launch in [Email 1], which includes performance improvements. The timeline provided by Dave is:
- February 17-21: Testing on staging
- February 24-25: Dry run on production replica
- March 1 (Saturday at 2 AM): Production upgrade, estimated downtime of four hours

Regarding team members involved in Falcon, Alice mentioned asking about Lisa Wong's first week in [Email 2].

Alice also requested Bob to check with Dave's team for the API keys necessary for integrating payment gateway functionalities as part of Project Falcon.

However, the marketing team requested a two-day delay, shifting the new launch date to March 17, 2025 as stated in [Email 2] sent by Alice Chen on February 13, 2025.

- February 17-21: Testing on staging
- February 24-25: Dry run on production replica
- March 1 (Saturday at 2 AM): Production upgrade, estimated downtime of four hours

Regarding team members involved in Falcon, Alice mentioned asking about Lisa Wong's first week in [Email 5].

Alice also requested Bob to check with Dave's team for the API keys necessary for integrating payment gateway functionalities as part of Project Falcon.

The marketing plan for Project Falcon's launch includes four phases:

Phase 1 (Mar 3-10): Teaser activities such as social media posts, blog articles, and an email blast.
Phase 2 (Mar 10-17): Pre-launch activities including a webinar, press releases, and partner collaborations.
Phase 3 (Mar 17): Launch day activities like posting on Product Hunt, releasing a demo video, and sending out an email blast.
Phase 4 (Mar 17-31): Post-launch activities involving case studies, customer testimonials, and paid advertisements.

The budget allocated for this plan is $25,000 ($8K ads, $7K content, $5K events, $5K tools).

This plan was sent to align with the project's new launch date of March 17, as requested by the marketing team in [Email 5].

Session tail (4 messages)
user:
Tell me about the production server outage.
assistant:
Production server us-east-1 went down at 3:05 AM UTC on February 11, 2025. The outage was caused by a connection leak in the nightly analytics batch job deployed Friday, which exhausted database connections and spiked CPU usage to 100%. Immediate actions included restarting the application server and bumping the pool limit to 200 connections to restore service temporarily. A permanent fix involving adding a finally block for proper connection release was then pushed to production by Dave Wilson at 2:30 PM UTC, fully resolving the issue. The postmortem document can be found here: https://wiki.acme.com/postmortem/2025-02-11 [Email 1].

[Email 1]

EMAILS REFERENCED ABOVE:
[Email 1] date=Tue, 11 Feb 2025 16:00:00 +0000 from=Dave Wilson <dave@acme.com> to=Bob Martinez <bob@acme.com>, Alice Chen <alice@acme.com> cc=Ops Team <ops@acme.com> subject=Re: URGENT: Production server down - RESOLVED + Postmortem
user:
How long was the outage and what was done to fix it?
assistant:
The production server outage lasted approximately 45 minutes with another 12 minutes of degraded service. To fix it, Dave Wilson restarted the application server and bumped the pool limit to 200 connections temporarily at 3:45 AM UTC. A permanent fix involving adding a finally block for proper connection release was then pushed by Dave Wilson at 2:30 PM UTC.

[Email 1], [Email 2], [Email 3]

[Email 2] date=Tue, 11 Feb 2025 03:45:00 +0000 from=Bob Martinez <bob@acme.com> to=Dave Wilson <dave@acme.com> cc=Ops Team <ops@acme.com> subject=Re: URGENT: Production server down - immediate action needed

user:
Was there a postmortem?
assistant:
Yes, there was a postmortem. According to [Email 1], the root cause of the production server outage was a connection leak in the nightly analytics batch job deployed on Friday, February 7th. The impact lasted for approximately 45 minutes with another 12 minutes of degraded service. A permanent fix involving adding a finally block for proper connection release was pushed to production at 2:30 PM UTC, resolving the issue fully.

The postmortem document can be found here: https://wiki.acme.com/postmortem/2025-02-11

Tell me about the BigCorp situation specifically.
assistant:
BigCorp reported API timeouts on POST /api/v2/orders starting Monday, affecting 15% of requests and resulting in daily losses of $20K. Sam Chen, CTO at BigCorp, demanded resolution by Friday to avoid CEO escalation.

The issue correlated with the nightly batch job that had consumed too many database connections, causing contention [Email 1]. The connection leak fix from Tuesday should resolve this. You asked BigCorp to monitor for 24 hours and offered to set up a dedicated connection pool if issues persist [Email 2].

Upon monitoring, the timeouts were fully resolved with success rates back at 99.97% over the last 48 hours after adding a dedicated connection pool and rate limit for BigCorp [Email 3]. Sam Chen thanked you for the quick resolution and acknowledged the improved confidence from the dedicated connection pool [Email 4].
